## 9. The Scale Test

**Question.** Can the plausible net revenue of the four families support about 190 made men (Part III 3.2 as first written: Sabatini 50 to 60, Pellegrino 45 to 55, Ferrante 40 to 50, Vannucci 30 to 40, with 5 to 8 associates per made man)? The result is reported as the model gave it. No input was changed to reach 190. Revision 2 restated the need at Part III 3.2 as revised (Pellegrino 40 to 50), so the midpoints are Sabatini 55, Pellegrino 45, Ferrante 45 and Vannucci 35, which is 180 men. **Revision 3 (October 2026) rebuilt this section from an explicit allocation table (9.3A), applied the audit's corrections and lowered the Pad (7.5). Every table below is recomputed.**

### 9.1 The short answer

1. **Revenue does not rule out 190 at the defaults. It also does not prove it.** At the Design defaults the four families net **$35.9M** a year after the Pad, about **$200,000** per claimed made man (180 men). At a default standard of living that supports about **360** made men, at a comfortable standard about **230**. About 180 to 190 needs **52 percent** of the default criminal revenue at the default standard, **81** percent at the comfortable one and **31** at the lean one (computed at 180).
2. **The defaults are mine, and the only outside evidence points down.** Reuter and Rubinstein (section 2.1) found numbers and bookmaking margins smaller than the popular picture and the Mafia a financier more than a controller. If the criminal sectors are half the default, about **170** men are supported at the default standard (**100** at the comfortable one). If they are at the low case, about **110** (**53** comfortable).
3. **Supportable range, default living standard: roughly 110 to 360. Central reading: 170 to 360. 190 sits just above the skeptic case, near the bottom of the central reading.** Leave Part III 3.2's total provisional. The omissions in 9.8 (violence, trials, forfeiture, leakage, laundering, attrition) are not netted and each lowers the count. **Read 360 as a ceiling, and the skeptic case, about 172 at the default standard before those costs, as the better guide.**
4. **Pellegrino is the weak link, with Ferrante close behind. Vannucci is the opposite.** See 9.5 and 9.7.
5. **The Ladder pays little.** Kick-ups under Part III 3.3 carry only about a fifth of what a family spends (9.6). Bosses live on their own rackets.

### 9.2 Method

A sector model of annual net revenue in 1975 dollars, early 1975, built from sections 2 to 8. Each sector is a default with a low and a high, all **Design** (the sources are in the sections). Revenue is allocated to the four families by a **Design** share per sector, **set out in full in 9.3A**. The Pad (section 7.5) is subtracted per family. The result is compared with a **required-spend model**: what the claimed number of made men and their associates spend at a given standard of living.

| Standard | Soldier | Capo | Each of 3 executives | Associate | Lawyers (family, a year) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Lean | $20,000 | $60,000 | $200,000 | $5,000 | $150,000 |
| **Default** | **$30,000** | **$90,000** | **$300,000** | **$7,500** | **$250,000** |
| Comfortable | $45,000 | $140,000 | $500,000 | $10,000 | $400,000 |

Other items: **associates per made man: Sabatini and Pellegrino 6.7, Ferrante 7.0, Vannucci 6.2, which is 1,202 at 180 men, inside Part III 3.2's 1,200 to 1,500. (Revision 2's correction used 6.5, 6.5, 7.0 and 6.0, which gave 1,175 and was still below the floor.)** One capo per eight men (at least two); prison and widow support of 8 percent of men at $7,800 a year; $600 a man for ceremonies and funerals (section 7.7). The default soldier income of $30,000 is 2.2 times the median family income of 1975 ($13,720). A soldier's good week of $1,500 (Part I 9.7) is 2.6 times his average.

Each Monte Carlo draw picks every sector from its low, default and high (a triangular draw), with a share of the draw common to all sectors (a correlation of 0, 0.5 or 0.8) so that a bad year is bad everywhere. **The Pad is drawn on its own from its range.**

**Corrections made to my own first run, disclosed.** (a) The first run had each dial step-2 family taxing the *whole* city drug market, which overstated drug income threefold ($4.8M against $1.46M). It now taxes only the family's own ground (section 6.5). (b) It charged the Pad only to the Vannucci, and my second run then charged the Vannucci's 14th Precinct payment twice. Each family now carries its Pad once. (c) It put the *bolita* tax at 60 percent of that game's net. (d) These changes cut the default total from $42.6M to $37.1M. (e) Revision 2 lowered the 1975 cocaine users from 4 to 2 per 1,000 after reading Part II 0.2 (6.2). That cut the default total to $37.0M. **(f) Revision 3: the numbers, loan and protection pools now leave the outside groups their shares (2.2, 3.2, 3.3); step-1 drug finance is taken on the family's own ground only (6.5); the Pad default falls from $2.7M to $1.6M (7.5); the associates rise to 1,202; and every allocation share is written down (9.3A). Net $35.9M; supportable men at the default 358.**

### 9.4 Results by case

Total supportable made men, all four families, sum of each family's maximum.

| Case | Net after Pad | Lean | Default | Comfortable |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Low (every sector at its low, Pad at its high) | **$14.0M** | **190** | **110** | **53** |
| **Skeptic (criminal sectors at 50 percent of default, fronts intact)** | **$19.7M** | **282** | **172** | **100** |
| **Default** | **$35.9M** | **558** | **358** | **230** |
| High | **$67.8M** | **1,096** | **718** | **488** |

The skeptic haircut of 50 percent was chosen before the run as a round number for a Reuter-style reading. It lands near 170 at the default standard. That is a coincidence of the numbers and not a tuning.

**The claimed size needs this much (spend for 160, 180 and 200 men at the three standards, the low, midpoint and high of Part III 3.2 as revised):**

| | 160 men | 180 men | 200 men |
|---|---|---|---|
| Lean | **$12.3M** | **$13.5M** | **$14.7M** |
| Default | **$18.5M** | **$20.3M** | **$22.0M** |
| Comfortable | **$27.0M** | **$29.6M** | **$32.0M** |

Per made man, to sustain a family at the three standards: **lean $75,000; default $113,000; comfortable $164,000.** The default revenue is **$200,000** per man, a cushion of **1.8** times against the default standard.

**Fraction of default criminal revenue needed to support the claimed size** (fronts and the Pad held, all four together, 180 men):

| Standard | Needed |
|---|---|
| Lean | **31 percent** |
| Default | **52 percent** |
| Comfortable | **81 percent** |

| Vannucci | 30 to 40 | **$11.1M** | **181** | **117** | **77** | **35% / 52%** |
| Ferrante | 40 to 50 | **$7.4M** | **109** | **69** | **43** | **66% / 103%** |

In the skeptic case (default standard) the figures are Sabatini **59**, Pellegrino **27**, Vannucci **53**, Ferrante **33**. In the low case: **33, 20, 40, 17**.

**Reading.**
- **Pellegrino.** At default revenue the claimed 40 to 50 is supported at the default standard or leaner (maximum **66**). At the comfortable standard a midpoint of 45 needs **107** percent of the default criminal revenue, and in the skeptic case the family supports 27, which is below even the lowered range. The Pellegrino count is the most exposed to any cut to the docks line (section 4.3) or to the fish market.
- **Ferrante.** **Close behind Pellegrino once its shares are written down (9.3A): maximum 69 at the default standard, and a midpoint of 45 needs 103 percent of its default criminal revenue at the comfortable standard. Its theft line (5.3) carries the most weight and the widest range.**
- **Vannucci.** They net the most per head (**$317,000**), more than the Sabatini, and their revenue would support **117** men against a claim of 30 to 40. Part III 4.4 described them as squeezed between the others, with the fewest men. Part III 4.4 now says they are squeezed on territory and not on income, a small, rich family with many associates (my call; 11.5). Their allocation is still the least secure line in the model: the carting line (89 percent of the $4.05M family pool to Vannucci) and the Strip line (78 percent of the $3.24M family pool) are Design.
- **Sabatini.** Supported at default standard and revenue. It fails the comfortable standard in the skeptic case (**36** of 50 to 60), as does Ferrante (**18** of 40 to 50).

### 9.6 The Ladder cross-check (Part III 3.3)

Part III 3.3: associates pay a soldier 1/5 to 1/4 of net; soldiers pay a capo 1/6 to 1/5; capos pay a boss 1/10 to 1/7. Midpoints 0.225, 0.183, 0.117. Sabatini at 55 men (45 soldiers, 7 capos, 3 executives, **369** associates), default standard:

| Pay-up | Per payer | Total |
|---|---|---|
| Associates to soldiers (each associate spends $7,500, so earns $9,677 before pay-up) | $2,177 | **$0.80M** |
| Soldiers to capos | $6,720 | $0.30M |
| Capos to the boss tier | $11,925 | $0.08M |
| **Ladder total** | | **$1.19M of $5.6M spent on men and associates (about $6.0M with lawyers, prison support and ceremonies), 21 percent (20 percent of the whole)** |

The three executives at the default standard spend $900,000 a year. The Ladder gives the boss tier about $83,000. The rest, over 90 percent, must be their own rackets and fronts: the Club, the docks, carting, the Strip. Tribute is a minor part of a family's income. The Ladder shows who owes whom. It does not explain where a boss's money comes from. A boss is a businessman, and his wealth rests on rackets he runs himself.

### 9.7 Distribution of results, and what it does not mean

Four thousand draws per row, net of the Pad, supportable made men (all four families), 10th, 50th and 90th percentiles. The shared share of each draw is the correlation.

| Correlation | Total net, $M | Lean | Default | Comfortable | Share of draws at 190 or more (comfortable) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | **35.9, 39.2, 42.7** | **556, 613, 671** | **356, 394, 433** | **230, 257, 285** | **100%** |
| 0.5 | **29.1, 39.0, 49.8** | **443, 610, 791** | **281, 392, 513** | **176, 255, 342** | **86%** |
| 0.8 | **27.0, 39.0, 52.6** | **408, 609, 840** | **256, 391, 546** | **159, 255, 365** | **80%** |

**The median draw sits above the default because most ranges run further above their default than below it (theft, $0.75M to $7.5M around $2.56M, is the widest).**

**Do not read these as probabilities that 190 is right.** The draws only spread my own Design ranges. They are centered on my defaults and cannot widen beyond them. The honest uncertainty is larger than the table: the real centre of the criminal revenue may be well under my default, which is why the skeptic and low cases above carry more weight than the draws.

**Sensitivity (default revenue, default standard, all four):**

| Change | Supportable |
|---|---|
| None | **358** |
| Carting removed | **310** |
| Strip removed | **320** |
| Cards and dice removed | **322** |
| All drugs removed | **351** |
| All legitimate fronts removed | **301** |
| Associate income $4,000 (was $7,500) | **484** |
| Associate income $12,000 | **267** |

**The hidden driver is the associate.** At **about 6.7** per made man **(1,202 in all)** they account for about 45 percent of required spend. An associate who lives mostly on a legitimate job and draws $4,000 from the Life makes 190 easy. A full-time associate at $12,000 still leaves **267**. Part III 3.2's 5 to 8 associates per made man is a Design count and should be read as "people who touch a made man's business", not as people he supports.

### 9.8 What the model leaves out

- The cost of violence, trials and forfeiture; the 1984 forfeiture law and later cases (section 10).
- Leakage upward: leaders skim.
- The made man's own legitimate work and his wife's.
- Income taxes and the cost of laundering (Part III 7.8).
- Freelance crime that is not family income.
- Made men who are not earners: old men, shelved men, and the boss's circle.
- The outside groups' share: they hold the Eastside heroin pool and the rest (section 8).
- Mortality and attrition: the roster is a stock, not a flow.

Each omission lowers the supportable count, except the made man's own work, which raises it. I did not try to net them.

### 9.9 Verdict and what was adjusted

Revision 1 listed eight edits to earlier Parts. **Revision 2 applied them. 11.5 gives the table.**

1. **Part III 3.2.** Kept as provisional and **now stated as Open and tied to revenue**: the engine draws a family's count inside its range from its revenue, and a revenue shock shrinks the count over years by attrition, not at once. The total now reads 180 to 190.
2. **Part III 3.2, Pellegrino.** **Default lowered to 40 to 50, default 45; a count above 50, up to 55, needs a dated cause (Part III 3.2). This was my call and Cade accepted it.** The family is the weakest per man (9.5). The alternative was to leave 45 to 55 and let the run find the weakness.
3. **Part III 4.4, Vannucci.** **Count kept at 30 to 40, and 4.4 rewritten as squeezed on territory and not on income. This was my call and Cade accepted it.** The alternatives were to raise the count toward 40 to 50 or to cut the carting and Strip shares. I took the option that did not reopen the family sizes.
4. **Part III 7.2.** The bank's net is now 3 to 15 cents on the dollar, default 8 (section 2.1).
5. **Part III 3.3.** Now says that the Ladder carries only about a fifth of a family's spending.
6. **Part I 9.7.** A soldier's ordinary week is about $600, his good week $1,500, and a capo's ordinary week nearer $1,700.
7. **Part V 9.2.** The Vannucci rationale is now risk appetite and the Strip's need for cash.
8. **Part I 9.1.** **Not a correction.** Revision 1 flagged the job total as high. That flag is withdrawn (1.3). Part I 9.1 now notes that the total counts jobs located in the city.

**Counterargument to the whole test.** A revenue model is a poor way to size a fictional organization. The Life's membership is set by history and honor, not by what the rackets earn, and the real families carried many more men than their income supported by letting soldiers work other jobs. The reading that follows is that 190 is a social fact first and an economic fact second. The model's hard messages are narrower: at the default standard the supportable count runs from about **110** (low case) to about **360** (default case), and a comfortable standard needs most of the default criminal revenue.

### 9.10 Open from this section

- The real centre of the criminal revenue. **Open** to the run, and the largest uncertainty in this Part.
- Whether made men carry the associates or the associates carry themselves. **Open**.
- The Vannucci question (9.9, item 3). **Open to the run**, with the accepted call as the default.

## 10. Price Weather, 1975 to 1990

**Weather** is a dated pressure that moves the numbers. Strength is weak, moderate or strong. The table gives the date, the pressure, its effect on the price book, its mark and what stays Open. It does not write the 1980 state. The run decides it, and none of these has to happen as listed (Open per run unless a date is a verified fact). **Weather is a pressure, never an outcome. Every number in the fourth column is a Design default response: it is sampled and logged like any Design figure, and applied only if the Weather fires and nothing in the run deflects it. A date marked [verified] or [verified in outline] fires; every other row is a pressure of the stated strength.**

**Counterargument.** A year-by-year table makes the future look scripted. Treat it as a mean path with a spread: the run may draw each series 20 to 30 percent either side, and a shock (a strike, a default, a federal case) can move a series by more.

## 11. Open Items, Anchors, Still to Verify, Consistency Check

### 11.1 New Open items

**Open** here means the engine or the run decides, within the range given.

- **Gambling.** The true numbers handle ($15M to $40M); whether the State's number is mostly rival or supplier (2.3); who replaces a failing bank (Part V 4.5); New Jersey's first-year Pick-3 sales; the Italian share of numbers and bookmaking (20 to 50 and 35 to 70 percent); whether a Saint Jude family may lend at the Atlantic City tables (2.6).
- **Loans and protection.** The collection rate (55 to 88 percent); the share of businesses that pay; whether any shark is a woman (3.5); whether the Club is a cartel or a dinner arrangement (3.4).
- **Labor.** The size of the Pellegrino docks flow ($1.4M to $4.8M); whether it holds under containerization; whether fund assets survive ERISA; whether Saint Jude's trucking locals sit in the same fund as the Central States case (4.6).
- **Theft.** The size of the Hunts Row dealer net ($2M to $9M); the number of loads (120 to 320) (5.7).
- **Drugs.** The share of each drug market on Italian ground (the single largest driver of family drug income); the shape of the crack market in the core; which family moves to dial step 3 on cocaine; whether Chinatown has a part in heroin (6.8). **Spend per user and the user counts are Design in every year, and the heroin path before 1981 is a back-extrapolation.**
- **Government.** The size of the Pad (**$0.9M to $2.7M**); whether the city defaults (7.9).
- **Outside groups.** Each group's flow is Design. Which family taxes the fuel scheme (8.3).
- **Scale.** The real centre of the criminal revenue; whether associates draw $4,000 or $12,000 from the Life; the Vannucci question (9.10).
- **Weather.** The date and strength of every Weather row that is not a verified fact.

### 11.5 Consistency check against Parts I to V

**Consistent and carried.** The four families, their territories and trades. The kick-ups on net through the Ladder. The 600 to 1 street payout and 40 percent hold (Part III 7.2). The shark rates (Part III 7.3). The Concrete Club at about 2 percent (Part III 7.4). The Part III 7.5 illustration of a fenced load (35 percent, 10 percent finder, about 10.7 percent costs). The Pick-3 date and its effect. The dial and its defaults (Part V 9.2), unchanged. The cross-river 50/50 as a cross-river share only. Payroll and benefits at three quarters of the city budget (Part IV 6.2). The fiscal year of 1 July. The Governor's appointment of judges and prosecutors. Women never inducted. Real people appear nowhere as actors. No method for any drug or weapon. No named secret. No em dashes.

**Conflicts found in revision 1, and what revision 2 did.** Each earlier-Part edit is bolded in that Part.

**Interpretations made, not contradictions.**
- The tax at dial step 2 is taken on the family's own ground only.
- The Pad is a cost against revenue, not a family income.
- Associates are counted as people the Life partly supports.
- "Net" means net to the family after its own costs but before the Ladder, which is a distribution rule.
- **"Net" for a business is after the owner-manager's own pay; "left for the owner" (Part IV 3.2) is before it (4.4).**

### 11.6 A note on method

The scale-test model is a Python script that I wrote and ran. Its inputs are the Design defaults and ranges in sections 2 to 8. It is reproducible from those tables but is not part of the Bible. Its first run had errors (9.2), which I found while drafting section 6 and corrected before the results in this Part. **In revision 2 I reran it for the drug market by year (6.3 and 6.5) and for the needed spend at the revised Pellegrino midpoint (9.1 to 9.5). Apart from the cocaine-user change in 9.2(e), no sector input changed, and the revenue, cases and Monte Carlo were rerun after that change (11.7).** **In revision 3 the script was rebuilt from the allocation table in 9.3A, which is now part of the Bible, so section 9 can be reproduced from the text alone.**
